    math.CT math.CO math.GT math.MG

    Categorical Tiling Theory: Constructing Directed Planar Tilings via Edge Reversal

    Authors: Catherine DiLeo, Preston Sessoms, Brandon T. Shapiro

    Abstract: Tilings of the plane resemble the simplicial and other complexes from algebraic topology, but have not been studied from this perspective. We construct finite categories corresponding to polygons with labeled directed edges, and introduce the problem of modeling tilings of the Euclidean or hyperbolic plane as presheaves over such a category. Combinatorially,… ▽ More Tilings of the plane resemble the simplicial and other complexes from algebraic topology, but have not been studied from this perspective. We construct finite categories corresponding to polygons with labeled directed edges, and introduce the problem of modeling tilings of the Euclidean or hyperbolic plane as presheaves over such a category. Combinatorially, this amounts to choosing an ``alignment'' for a tiling: a direction for every edge and consistent labels for the edges of each polygonal tile. We show that for a fixed tiling, given a single alignment we can characterize every other alignment of the same tiling by comparison of the edge directions. We then construct a ``reflective'' alignment for any tiling with an even number of polygons at each vertex, and from this generate a large family of alignments with elegant symmetry properties. △ Less

    Submitted 28 October, 2026; v1 submitted 8 September, 2025; originally announced September 2025.

    Comments: 38 pages + 4 page appendix. v2: Minor edits and corrections, accepted for publication at Involve

    MSC Class: 18F99 (Primary) 52C20; 18F20; 05C10; 51F15; 51M10 (Secondary)

    math.PR

    Well-posedness of Rough Differential Equations at the Krylov-Röckner Integrability Threshold

    Authors: Zimo Hao, Khoa Lê

    Abstract: …the Itô rough path lift of the Brownian motion, such an RDE has a unique solution for every initial value. In other words, for almost every realization of the Itô lift, the corresponding rough differential equation has exactly one solution in the class of Davie-type solutions, for every initial value; this is the appropriate notion of path-by-path uniqueness… ▽ More We study rough differential equations (RDEs) driven by Brownian (Itô) rough paths with uniformly locally integrable drifts. The diffusion coefficient is assumed to be uniformly elliptic, of class $C^{2+}$ in space and of bounded variation in time, while the drift is $q$-integrable in time and $p$-integrable in space, uniformly on balls of a fixed radius, for some $p,q$ satisfying the Krylov--Röckner condition $\frac dp+\frac2q<1$. We show that, for almost every realization of the Itô rough path lift of the Brownian motion, such an RDE has a unique solution for every initial value. In other words, for almost every realization of the Itô lift, the corresponding rough differential equation has exactly one solution in the class of Davie-type solutions, for every initial value; this is the appropriate notion of path-by-path uniqueness for the corresponding stochastic differential equation (SDE) with multiplicative noise. The proof follows the strategy of Shaposhnikov (2016). The principal new ingredients are the stability of RDEs with bounded variation perturbation and Davie-type estimates for the flow of the driftless equation with non-constant diffusion coefficients, which we derive from known maximal regularity estimates for the corresponding parabolic partial differential equations (PDEs). △ Less

    MSC Class: 60H10; 60L20; 60H50; 60J60

  3. arXiv:2610.39147  [pdf, ps, other] 

    cs.CV

    Kinematic Causal Attribution: Black-Box Explanations of Motion versus Geometry for 4D Point-Cloud Video Models

    Authors: Zerui Wang

    Abstract: …attribution is confined to RGB pixels, gradient maps need white-box access, attention is absent from state-space backbones, and point-level perturbations assume a point correspondence across frames that real depth and LiDAR data lack. We present Kinematic Causal Attribution (KCA), a black-box, interventional attribution method that queries only the model'… ▽ More 4D perception models, which classify 3D point-cloud videos, are being built into robots, assistive monitoring and AR, yet before deploying one a practitioner cannot check which cue it relies on: how a body part moves, or what shape it has. 3D attribution is single-frame, video attribution is confined to RGB pixels, gradient maps need white-box access, attention is absent from state-space backbones, and point-level perturbations assume a point correspondence across frames that real depth and LiDAR data lack. We present Kinematic Causal Attribution (KCA), a black-box, interventional attribution method that queries only the model's outputs. Its freeze operator removes a region's motion while keeping its shape, and a shape-free proxy removes the shape while keeping the motion. Together they span a motion$\times$geometry cube for every tracked region, whose order-symmetric Shapley decomposition is complete and whose interaction is measured rather than assumed away. The operators rewrite per-frame point sets, so they need no correspondences, and a context-averaged estimator handles structures that span several regions. We validate KCA on the official PSTNet, P4Transformer and Mamba4D trained on MSR-Action3D. The decisive test is an audit: two binary tasks are built from real clips whose classes differ only in the motion or only in the shape of the same arm, and the motion share of the arm's importance follows the defining cue on all three backbones (0.83 vs. 0.60 on P4Transformer, 0.86 vs. 0.30 on PSTNet, 0.92 vs. 0.61 on Mamba4D), a distinction no single attribution map can make. The split costs no faithfulness: the necessity maps have the lowest deletion AUC on P4Transformer and PSTNet, tie with the best baseline on Mamba4D, and lose their correlation with the trained model's once the weights are randomised. Skeleton ground truth, re-sampling stability and cost complete the validation. △ Less

    Submitted 28 October, 2026; originally announced October 2026.

    Comments: 27 pages, 6 figures, 14 tables

    ACM Class: I.2.10; I.2.6

  4. arXiv:2512.24512  [pdf, ps, other] 

    physics.flu-dyn physics.ao-ph

    The electrostatic-hydrodynamic interaction of two spheres in a linear compressional flow

    Authors: Pijush Patra, Srikumar Warrier, Anubhab Roy

    Abstract: …the particle size and charge ratios. We identify a critical electrostatic strength above which particles approaching from the far field cannot reach contact. This threshold corresponds to a saddle-node bifurcation of the relative-trajectory field on the compressional axis. Below this threshold, near-field polarization-induced attraction can increase the upst… ▽ More We investigate the collision dynamics of two like-charged dielectric spheres in a uniaxial compressional flow, accounting for non-continuum lubrication interactions, van der Waals attraction, and the complete finite-dielectric electrostatic interaction. Mutual polarization causes the electrostatic force to behave substantially differently from the point-charge approximation and can produce strong near-field attraction despite far-field Coulomb repulsion, depending on the particle size and charge ratios. We identify a critical electrostatic strength above which particles approaching from the far field cannot reach contact. This threshold corresponds to a saddle-node bifurcation of the relative-trajectory field on the compressional axis. Below this threshold, near-field polarization-induced attraction can increase the upstream interception area and substantially enhance the collision efficiency. We report that electrostatic interactions lead to non-monotonic variations of collision efficiency with electrostatic strength, particle size ratio, and charge ratio. When electrostatic interactions remain repulsive at all separations, increasing their strength suppresses collisions monotonically. Non-continuum lubrication and van der Waals interactions further modify the collision efficiency, although sufficiently strong polarization-induced attraction can dominate these near-field effects. For weak electrostatic interactions, a perturbation analysis expresses the leading correction to the collision efficiency as a weighted average of the electrostatic force along the limiting colliding trajectory, with the weight concentrated in the lubrication region; this prediction agrees closely with the numerical results. These results demonstrate that finite-size polarization and near-field hydrodynamic interactions jointly govern the collision behaviour of like-charged particles in compressional flows. △ Less

    Comments: 31 pages and 10 figures

    Convergence rates of mixed primal-dual dynamical systems with implicit Hessian damping and Tikhonov regularization

    Authors: Hong-lu Li, Yi-bin Xiao, Xin He, Kequan Zhao

    Abstract: We propose a mixed-order primal--dual dynamical system with implicit Hessian damping and Tikhonov regularization for linearly constrained convex optimization, and derive a corresponding fast primal--dual algorithm. The proposed dynamical system couples a second-order primal equation with a first-order dual equation without requiring twice differentiability o… ▽ More We propose a mixed-order primal--dual dynamical system with implicit Hessian damping and Tikhonov regularization for linearly constrained convex optimization, and derive a corresponding fast primal--dual algorithm. The proposed dynamical system couples a second-order primal equation with a first-order dual equation without requiring twice differentiability of the objective function. An energy analysis establishes $\mathcal{O}(t^{-2})$ convergence rates for the augmented Lagrangian gap, feasibility violation, and objective residual along an extrapolated primal trajectory. For the proposed algorithm, we construct discrete energy estimates and establish the corresponding $\mathcal{O}(k^{-2})$ rates at the extrapolated primal iterates. In particular, in the critical regularization regimes $ε(t)=a/t^2$ and $ε_k=a/k^2$, these accelerated rates hold simultaneously with strong convergence of the primal trajectory and primal iterates to the minimum-norm solution, respectively, under suitable parameter and trajectory conditions. Numerical experiments illustrate the convergence behavior of the proposed dynamical system and the corresponding algorithm. △ Less

We also discuss the relevance of heavy-quark spin symme… ▽ More We investigate a doubly charmed molecular state $T_{cc\bar{s}}$ with $C=+2$, $\mathcal{S}=+1$, and $I=1/2$ in an off-shell coupled-channel formalism. Constructing the interaction kernel from a meson-exchange model based on an effective Lagrangian and solving the coupled-channel scattering equation in the Blankenbecler-Sugar reduction, we find a pole only for $J^P=1^+$ among $J^P=0^\pm$, $1^\pm$, and $2^\pm$. With the reduced cutoff mass of $600$~MeV, we obtain a mass of $3972.90$~MeV, which corresponds to a binding energy of $4.01$~MeV relative to the $D^*D_s$ threshold. Examining the relative signs and magnitudes of the $S$-wave channel couplings extracted from the pole residues, we demonstrate that the pole belongs predominantly to the triplet ($\mathbf{3}_A$) representation. We also discuss the relevance of heavy-quark spin symmetry to the bound state. △ Less

    Beyond energy bounds: comparing Schrödinger eigenstates

    Authors: Xiao-Ke Wang, Han Jia, Shengjun Wu, Zeng-Bing Chen

    Abstract: …right Dirichlet walls, with coordinatewise comparisons for submodular reference potentials and coordinatewise nondecreasing perturbations. A two-sided Sturm argument orders corresponding internal nodes for nondecreasing potential differences, even between orthogonal one-dimensional excited states. For Yukawa ground states, Hulthén comparisons bound overlaps,… ▽ More We derive eigenstate comparisons from ordered Schrödinger operators, extending energy bounds to overlaps, spatial distributions and nodes. For three form-ordered operators with discrete comparison ground states, suppose the upper ground-state energy lies in the lower operator's ground-state gap. Three comparison spectral values then bound all three squared ground-state overlaps and guarantee a simple discrete intermediate ground state. Related gap estimates control low-energy subspaces and individual excited-state overlaps under additional gap conditions. In one dimension and radially, a single crossing of the potential difference relative to the energy shift orders ground-state likelihood ratios and monotone observables; a nondecreasing difference suffices. The results cover asymmetric wells and shortened right Dirichlet walls, with coordinatewise comparisons for submodular reference potentials and coordinatewise nondecreasing perturbations. A two-sided Sturm argument orders corresponding internal nodes for nondecreasing potential differences, even between orthogonal one-dimensional excited states. For Yukawa ground states, Hulthén comparisons bound overlaps, radial moments and the density at the nucleus; the latter two-sided bounds hold for screening $0<δ<2/\sqrt6$. Optimizing scaled Hulthén majorants gives a lower bound of approximately $1.13611$ for $n^2δ_c(n,0)$, where $δ_c(n,0)$ is the critical screening for the $n$th $s$-wave bound state. This bound is optimal within that two-parameter comparison family. △ Less

    Muon-to-positron conversion from light Majorana neutrino exchange: nuclear responses and pion-pole-improved multipoles

    Authors: Yi Liao, Hao-Lin Wang

    Abstract: …The two physical time orderings and the distinct momentum transfers are derived in the intermediate-state response. We complete its angular reduction and derive the corresponding closure operator, including spin-gradient terms. Both the symmetric (scalar) and antisymmetric (vector) lepton-hadron Lorentz contractions contribute to the spin-averaged, angle-in… ▽ More We study muon-to-positron conversion in nuclei that is mediated by light Majorana neutrinos, which probes lepton-number violation in the electron-muon sector. As the second paper in a series, this work extends our study of local dimension-nine interactions to the mass mechanism. We derive the conversion amplitude from the second-order effective four-Fermi interaction and express it in terms of nuclear responses in the one-body impulse approximation. Using phenomenological nucleon form factors, we retain convection, axial charge, weak magnetism, and the longitudinal pion-pole current in a specified pion-pole-improved prescription. The two physical time orderings and the distinct momentum transfers are derived in the intermediate-state response. We complete its angular reduction and derive the corresponding closure operator, including spin-gradient terms. Both the symmetric (scalar) and antisymmetric (vector) lepton-hadron Lorentz contractions contribute to the spin-averaged, angle-integrated rate. For $0^+\!\to0^+$ transitions we give explicit formulas in terms of two coherent radial amplitudes, without restricting the intermediate nuclei to spin zero. This provides an interface to nuclear calculations of the required nuclear matrix elements. However, in this work, no isotope-specific numerical predictions are attempted. The present treatment uses approximate lepton wavefunctions and recoil-free nuclear propagation. Two-body weak currents and systematic chiral effective-field-theory matching are left for future work. △ Less

    Finiteness of planar central configurations of five bodies for all positive masses: On Smale's sixth problem

    Authors: Zhe Chen, Zhicheng Tong

    Abstract: …of positive masses. This gives the five-body statement of Smale's sixth problem. We use the singular-sequence classification of Albouy and Kaloshin and resolve the corresponding boundary equations through weighted blow-up charts, rank calculations and local compatibility conditions. For the two remaining diagrams, we prove that the product $Δ$ of all squ… ▽ More Combining local boundary analysis, algebraic curves and the computational statements specified in the appendix, we prove that the planar Newtonian five-body problem has finitely many central configurations up to rotation, translation and scaling for every choice of positive masses. This gives the five-body statement of Smale's sixth problem. We use the singular-sequence classification of Albouy and Kaloshin and resolve the corresponding boundary equations through weighted blow-up charts, rank calculations and local compatibility conditions. For the two remaining diagrams, we prove that the product $Δ$ of all squared mutual distances vanishes. On a hypothetical solution curve containing a positive arc, a pole of a four-product therefore gives a zero of $Δ$. A pole of $Δ$ elsewhere on the completed curve, together with $Δ^4=\prod_{i<j}p_{ij}$, then contradicts the same local vanishing result. The computational statements for Diagrams~2, 3 and~6 involve exact algebra, interval arithmetic and ball arithmetic. △ Less

    Clean Sheets and Checkpoints: Evidence Complexity for Certifying Sequential Agent Risk

    Authors: Rohith Yanambaka Venkata, Don McBride, Tiago Sequeira, Md. Al Amin

    Abstract: …bound also covers adaptive deployment processes that follow the chain's transitions, provided their success probabilities after every complete history are capped by the corresponding audit success probabilities. The audit does not test this transport assumption. In rare-risk phase interiors, each segment is either rarely completed within the budget (acti… ▽ More Dangerous-capability evaluations often test an AI agent from prepared states at ordered milestones. A clean sheet records no successes. What it certifies about a campaign of $B$ attempts depends on what a failed attempt erases. We model the task as a chain of $k$ milestones divided into checkpoint segments and ask how many zero audit responses certify, at level $α$, a completion probability of at most $ε$. For segments chosen before the audit, counting possible completion windows gives a certificate for any specified budget. The bound also covers adaptive deployment processes that follow the chain's transitions, provided their success probabilities after every complete history are capped by the corresponding audit success probabilities. The audit does not test this transport assumption. In rare-risk phase interiors, each segment is either rarely completed within the budget (active) or nearly always completed (free). The minimum expected number of zero responses is asymptotic to $\log(1/α)\,(B^a/(a!\,ε))^{1/K_A}$, where the $a$ active segments contain $K_A$ milestones and are those at least as long as a budget-dependent threshold. The certificate attains this constant. Every asymptotically optimal evaluator must divide its expected response count equally among active milestones in the limit, with a vanishing share elsewhere. Where a single segment passes from active to free, each of its milestones receives a smaller limiting share than an active milestone; a crossover-aware certificate attains the optimal constant. For selected segments with known or unqueried prefix steps, counting failed cycles can sharpen the certificate. △ Less

    A Low-Dimensional Chemical Representation of High-Resolution Stellar Disk Spectra: Residual Abundance Signatures

    Authors: Sarah E. Aquilina, Melissa K. Ness, Sven Buder

    Abstract: …reproduces the global spectra to a median residual amplitude of 0.3%. Deviations in 15 absorption features for unlabelled elements are typically < 0.7%. Residuals of ~1% correspond to abundance differences of 0.01-0.05 dex. Degrading the spectra to medium resolution (R = 28,000) decreases the median residual amplitude from 0.3% to 0.2%, redistributing the… ▽ More Stellar abundances are generated by complex chemical enrichment pathways yet show strong correlation structure. We test how well high-quality spectra of low-alpha disk stars can be reconstructed using low-dimensional models. By validating model performance in flux space, we quantify and locate where residual information persists. Using HARPS spectra (R = 115,000, SNR > 100) and labels derived with Korg, we train generative models (The Cannon) conditioned on stellar parameters Teff, log(g), [M/H], vsini, and vmic, and three abundance labels, [Ti/H], [Y/H], and [Nd/H], representing distinct nucleosynthetic families. Evaluating model performance on unseen test stars through flux residuals, the model reproduces the global spectra to a median residual amplitude of 0.3%. Deviations in 15 absorption features for unlabelled elements are typically < 0.7%. Residuals of ~1% correspond to abundance differences of 0.01-0.05 dex. Degrading the spectra to medium resolution (R = 28,000) decreases the median residual amplitude from 0.3% to 0.2%, redistributing the residual signal over the instrumental line profile. Our publicly available model reproduces features from 29 elements to sub-percent precision across -1.13 < [M/H] < 0.29. This demonstrates that the dominant chemical variation encoded in low-alpha disk spectra can be captured by a low-dimensional model, while weaker chemical information remains encoded in flux residuals. High-resolution, high-SNR spectra (R ~ 100,000, SNR > 100) are essential to resolve the hierarchical layers of chemical information beyond this low-dimensional structure. The model provides a benchmark for assessing the chemical information accessible to current surveys like 4MOST and GALAH 2, as well as upcoming facilities like VLT/HRMOS and GMT/G-CLEF. △ Less

    Hermitian Clifford Analysis on Euclidean Domains with Smooth and Fractal Boundaries: A Survey of Matrix Cauchy and Hilbert Transforms, Boundary Value Problems, Helmholtz-Type Dirac Operators and Duality

    Abstract: …prerequisites (a Poincaré lemma and a Mittag--Leffler theorem for circulant matrices); the function theory associated with a Hermitian version of the Helmholtz equation, the corresponding $\bar\partial$-problem and its application to a formal two-dimensional Maxwell-type system; and finally, the parallel development in the quaternionic Hermitian setting, whe… ▽ More Hermitian Clifford analysis is a refinement of Euclidean Clifford analysis obtained by imposing a complex structure on $\R^{2n}$ and considering the simultaneous null solutions (the $h$-monogenic functions) of two mutually adjoint Hermitian Dirac operators.     math.DS

    The Collatz Conjecture & Non-Archimedean Spectral Theory: Part I -- Arithmetic Dynamical Systems and Non-Archimedean Value Distribution Theory

    Authors: Maxwell Charles Siegel

    Abstract: …) and prove the Correspondence Principle (CP): $x\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\left\{ 0\right\}$ is a periodic point of $T_{q}$ if and only there is a $\mathfrak{z}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}\backslash\left\{ 0,1,2,\ldots\right\}$ so that $χ_{q}\left(\mathfrak{z}\right)=x$. ▽ More Let $q$ be an odd prime, and let $T_{q}:\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}$ be the Shortened $qx+1$ map, defined by $T_{q}\left(n\right)=n/2$ if $n$ is even and $T_{q}\left(n\right)=\left(qn+1\right)/2$ if $n$ is odd. The study of the dynamics of these maps is infamous for its difficulty, with the characterization of the dynamics of $T_{3}$ being an alternative formulation of the famous Collatz Conjecture.     Estimating the Kinetic Energy Spectrum from the Second-Order Velocity Structure Function using a Regularized Fitting Approach

    Authors: Ayantika Bhattacharjee, Spencer Jones, Dhruv Balwada, Shane Elipot, Manuel Gutierrez-Villanueva

    Abstract: …problem. The approach is first validated in an idealized setting; for a number of idealized KE spectra with prescribed sets of spectral slopes and amplitudes, the corresponding structure functions are computed by numerically evaluating the forward relationship. These structure functions are then used to determine the underlying parameters using our proposed… ▽ More Ocean turbulence plays a key role in shaping large-scale circulation, heat uptake, and biogeochemical processes. The kinetic energy (KE) wavenumber spectrum is a fundamental diagnostic, quantifying how KE is distributed across spatial scales. The second-order structure function -- computed from velocity differences between spatially separated observations -- provides a complementary measure, but unlike the KE spectrum, it reflects a non-local, weighted integral of KE over all scales. Analytic relationships link the two metrics, permitting forward and inverse transformations between them. However, recovering the KE spectrum from the structure function via the inverse relationship is highly sensitive to sampling limitations and numerical discretization errors. Here we propose a regularized approach in which the spectrum is assumed to consist of a finite number of segments with distinct slopes and amplitudes, and the inversion is formulated as an optimization problem. The approach is first validated in an idealized setting; for a number of idealized KE spectra with prescribed sets of spectral slopes and amplitudes, the corresponding structure functions are computed by numerically evaluating the forward relationship. These structure functions are then used to determine the underlying parameters using our proposed approach, which shows that we are able to perfectly recover the parameters and consequently the KE spectra. The method is further evaluated on high-resolution ocean model output, where it reconstructs the underlying spectra well even in the presence of noise. Finally, we apply the method to surface drifter observations (GLAD and LASER experiments). The results show that the framework enables estimation of the KE spectrum from sparse Lagrangian data, extending spectral diagnostics beyond gridded Eulerian measurements. △ Less

    BASILISC: Bayesian Alignment of Siamese-Integrated Latents for Inferring Single-cell Correspondence

    Authors: Sepideh Saran, Mahsa Ghanbari, Uwe Ohler

    Abstract: …layer, the newer multimodal assays measure several. Most data are single-modality, so integrating them relies on models trained on multimodal assays, where the cell correspondence across modalities is known. These models return a matching, but rarely a confidence measure. We present BASILISC (Bayesian Alignment of Siamese-Integrated Latents for Inferring Sin… ▽ More Single-cell assays study the genome one cell at a time. Unimodal assays measure one molecular layer, the newer multimodal assays measure several. Most data are single-modality, so integrating them relies on models trained on multimodal assays, where the cell correspondence across modalities is known. These models return a matching, but rarely a confidence measure. We present BASILISC (Bayesian Alignment of Siamese-Integrated Latents for Inferring Single-cell Correspondence), a framework for matching single-cell representations with uncertainty on the correspondence itself. A BASILISC model is a probabilistic siamese network over the two modalities with a predictive distribution over the correspondence for a cross-modal pair of cells. We instantiate it at two levels: at the cell-type level the question is whether two cells share a cell type, at the cell level which cell is the partner in the other modality. To our knowledge, this is the first uncertainty score on a learned cross-modal correspondence evaluated against a known ground-truth pairing. BASILISC learns a similarity across modalities that reflects cell lineage: an unseen cell type is matched to its lineage relative, matching still works on such held-out types (balanced accuracy 0.86 on unseen against 0.93 on seen types), and the predictive uncertainty rises on these out-of-distribution cells. The uncertainty score also selects high-quality matches for downstream analysis: discarding the least certain fifth of the pairs raises accuracy from 0.93 to 0.98. At the cell level, a deep ensemble of the deterministic matcher reaches 0.062 matching accuracy on the benchmark test set, above the competition winner; on the Bayesian matcher's assignment, the posterior flags the wrong matches that cross a cell-type boundary better than a deterministic read of the same scores (AUROC 0.68 against 0.64). △ Less

    Wide Field Localization Using Fixed Modulation Collimators

    Authors: Steven E. Boggs

    Abstract: …of these count rates robustly cancels detector background and grid leakage without requiring rotation. Because the fringe pattern repeats across the sky, a phase measurement corresponds to many candidate source positions; we resolve this ambiguity over an arbitrarily wide field of view using a cascade of progressively coarser-pitched grid stages, generalizin… ▽ More Rapid, precise localization of gamma-ray bursts (GRBs) requires continuous wide-field imaging at hard X-ray and soft gamma-ray energies. We develop a fixed (non-rotating) modulation-collimator architecture to address this challenge. A bigrid collimator, a pair of aligned, periodic slit grids in front of a simple, non-imaging photon-counting detector, transmits an off-axis source through a triangular fringe pattern whose local angular position, the source phase, is recovered from simultaneous count rates in several grid pairs at fixed relative phase offsets, rather than by mechanically rotating a single grid pair as in the classical rotating modulation collimator. Four-phase demodulation of these count rates robustly cancels detector background and grid leakage without requiring rotation. Because the fringe pattern repeats across the sky, a phase measurement corresponds to many candidate source positions; we resolve this ambiguity over an arbitrarily wide field of view using a cascade of progressively coarser-pitched grid stages, generalizing the two-grid vernier-scale technique originally used to localize Sco X-1. This vernier cascade technique reduces the required photon counts and grid-calibration accuracy required to achieve fine angular resolution over a broad field of view. Generalized to two dimensions with orthogonal cascades, the resulting architecture achieves wide-field localization with no moving parts, no time modulation, and only simple photon-counting detectors, a promising candidate for simple, inexpensive, continuous-monitoring instruments supporting time-domain and multi-messenger astrophysics. △ Less

Information-plane analyses suggest that neural networks can traverse representations substantially more complex than those required by their final predictors. We explore when this transient complexity serves a computational purpose by comparing the representation complexity needed for prediction with that needed to make an unknown target statistically visible through gradient feedback. We introduce an oracle model of neural network learning and use Kolmogorov \(n\)-widths to quantify effective representation complexity. Our main technical result transfers complexity bounds from the forward representation to the corresponding gradient-query features. Combined with statistical-query lower bounds, this gives a task-agnostic lower bound on the initial effective width required for target-dependent gradient information to become detectable. For \(k\)-sparse parity in \(D\) dimensions, a known target admits an \(O(k)\)-width representation, while the general bound already implies a polynomial separation between representation and discovery. We then exploit parity's invariance under coordinatewise sign reflections to identify a sharper unsigned effective width, which removes sign distinctions invisible to the parity information channel. At a transfer-compatible measurement resolution, its critical scale is \(\widetildeΘ_k\left( D^{k-1}τ^2 \right)\), up to logarithmic factors. Thus, the representation resources required to discover a predictor can substantially exceed those required to represent it once learned. Interpreting precision through accumulation over fresh samples yields a corresponding width-time frontier. Experiments support the predicted dimension dependence and examine how optimisation and generalisation depart from this idealised frontier. △ Less

We represent each function in an $L_1$ ball by a probability measure of controlled relative entropy, and this entropy pays for the corrections to the predictions of its coefficients. Processing the thresholds from large to small, we obtain a single weighted bound for all the corrections, which we turn into a finite description of approximate coefficient squares and then into a conditional Rademacher estimate, and symmetrization completes the argument directly on the $L_1$ ball. Our formalism enables us to separate the black hole mass function into sub-populations corresponding to mergers between objects formed via different astrophysical pathways, allowing us to infer the properties of black holes formed from stellar collapse and black holes formed via prior mergers separately. Applying this formalism, we find that this model fits the data better than the powerlaw+peak model with Bayes factor $ 9.7\pm0.1$. We measure the location of the lower edge of the upper black hole mass gap to be $M_{\rm BHMG}=84.05_{-12.88}^{+17.19}{\rm M}_{\odot}$, providing evidence that the $35{\rm M}_{\odot}$ Gaussian peak detected in the data using other models is not associated with the PPISN pile-up predicted to precede this gap. Incorporating spin, we find that the normalized spins of stellar remnant black holes are close to zero while those of higher generation black holes tend to larger values. All of these results are in accordance with the predictions of stellar structure theory and black hole merger scenarios. Finally, we combine our mass function with the spectral siren method for measuring the Hubble constant to find $H_0=36.19_{-10.91}^{17.50}$ km/s/Mpc and discuss potential explanations of this low value. Our results demonstrate how astrophysically-informed mass functions can facilitate the interpretation of gravitational wave catalog data to provide information about black hole formation and cosmology. Future data releases will improve the precision of our measurements. △ Less

    Self-Supervised Doppler-Guided RF Odometry

    Authors: Kunzhe Song, Jialuo Du, Jingkai Lin, Huacheng Zeng

    Abstract: Radio-frequency (RF) sensing enables robot ego-… ▽ More Radio-frequency (RF) sensing enables robot ego-motion estimation in darkness, smoke, and fog, yet RF odometry remains challenging because multipath and moving reflectors make cross-frame matching highly ambiguous. We present RFWalk, a self-supervised, RF-only odometry framework that models dense correspondences across range-azimuth frames as random walks on a space-time graph and learns them from unlabeled sequences through temporal cycle consistency. The bid- and ask-side order densities are modelled as stochastic processes with values in infinite-dimensional function spaces that solve stochastic partial differential equations (SPDEs) with stochastic order submission rates and multiplicative infinite-dimensional noise. Trading activity affects the order densities, which determine the market depths near the best quotes and hence the mid-price, and the mid-price in turn affects the traders' decisions. We then study the corresponding mean field game as the number of traders tends to infinity, characterize the mean field equilibrium as a fixed point of the associated consistency condition, and prove its existence and uniqueness under suitable conditions. Finally, we show that the mean field equilibrium induces an $ε_N$-Nash equilibrium for the finite-player game, with $ε_N=O(N^{-1/2})$, and provide a numerical illustration of the equilibrium dynamics. △ Less

    Authors: Hongyi Chen, Seoyeon Yang

    Authors: S Mahmudul Hasan, Lu Peng

    Abstract: …datasets, and 26 models. Our results show that capture-chain corruption… ▽ More Crowd-counting models deployed on edge devices face two challenges: inputs are degraded along the capture and transmission pipeline, while models are often quantized to satisfy resource constraints of the edge. Yet corruption robustness and deployment quantization are typically evaluated separately. We introduce CrowdCC, a controlled benchmark that jointly evaluates these effects across 23 deployment-motivated corruptions at three severity levels, four crowd-counting datasets, and 26 models. We evaluate TensorRT engines at FP32, FP16, and post-training INT8 precision on an A100 GPU, alongside throughput and energy measurements on corresponding Jetson Orin~NX configurations. The corruptions span six families, with head annotations adjusted when geometry or visibility changes. Our results show that capture-chain corruptions consistently cause the largest degradation across evaluated models, while crowd density affects absolute and relative errors differently. Accuracy on clean images does not reliably predict performance on the corresponding corrupted images. INT8 changes corruption robustness more than FP16, without a consistent direction, and does not consistently improve latency or energy efficiency. INT8 inference efficiency is strongly associated with reformat overhead in the compiled engine, with slowdowns also observed under limited INT8 coverage. Precision changes corrupted-error rankings and robustness--cost frontiers, highlighting the need to consider corruption robustness and deployment cost jointly when selecting models and precision. The code is available at https://github.com/Tulane-Computer-Architecture-Systems/CrowdCC-Release. △ Less

    Efficient Agentic Audio-Visual Question Answering through Toolset Pruning and Modality Consolidation

    Authors: Yi Wang, Tianzi Wang, Peter Bell

    Abstract: Fine-grained audio-visual question answering (AVQA) requires models to resolve complex cross-modal correspondences and temporally grounding from sparsely distributed evidence. Omni-modal agents, which autonomously orchestrate perception tools to actively collect evidence, achieve strong performance, but it remains unclear whether active perception from both… ▽ More Fine-grained audio-visual question answering (AVQA) requires models to resolve complex cross-modal correspondences and temporally grounding from sparsely distributed evidence. Omni-modal agents, which autonomously orchestrate perception tools to actively collect evidence, achieve strong performance, but it remains unclear whether active perception from both audio and visual modalities depends on broad specialized toolsets, especially audio or visual modality-specific tools. Such systems may also introduce long execution trajectories and substantial inference cost. We investigate whether comparable performance can be retained with a compact workflow exposing only essential perceptual capabilities. Starting from the specialized-tool OmniAgent, we conduct staged controlled experiments that prune the toolset and progressively consolidate modality-specific tools into omni-modal tools, motivating a two-tool agent comprising only Omni-modal captioning and Omni-modal QA. Across three widely used AVQA benchmarks, our compact agent achieves comparable or better accuracy than the specialized-tool agent while reducing inference cost by around 70%. △ Less

    Authors: Nikolas Castro

    Abstract: …. We study the Fargues-Scholze categorical Langlands correspondence at generic parameters $φ$, namely those whose semisimplification does not coincide with that of any parameter with nontrivial monodromy. Extending the theory of quasi-exceptional sets on the nilpotent cone developed by Bezrukavnikov to the setting of disconnected reductive groups which arise… ▽ More Let $G$ be a quasi-split reductive group over a finite extension $F$ of $\mathbb{Q}_p$. We study the Fargues-Scholze categorical Langlands correspondence at generic parameters $φ$, namely those whose semisimplification does not coincide with that of any parameter with nontrivial monodromy. Extending the theory of quasi-exceptional sets on the nilpotent cone developed by Bezrukavnikov to the setting of disconnected reductive groups which arise as stabilizers of semisimple generic parameters, to any irreducible representation $ρ$ of such a group we construct sheaves labeled $\overline{\nabla}_ρ$, $\overlineΔ_ρ$, $IC_ρ$, and $δ_ρ$ on the moduli stack of L-parameters for $G$. Using forthcoming work of Hamann, Hansen, and Mann on geometric Eisenstein series compatibility, the (conjectural, in general) behavior of the correspondence at generous parameters, and the (conjectural, in general) agreement between Fargues-Scholze and classical semisimple L-parameters we prove that these sheaves correspond under the Fargues-Scholze functor to the sheaves $i_{b!}π$, $i_{b\sharp}π$, $i_{b\sharp!}π$, and $i_{b!*}π$ on $\text{Bun}_G$, where $π$ is a smooth irreducible representation with Fargues-Scholze parameter $φ$. As applications we prove t-exactness properties of the Fargues-Scholze functor specialized at a generic parameter, and that Hecke operators are perverse t-exact on the generic locus. We also construct and study foundational properties of Hecke eigensheaves at generic parameters, compute non-semisimplified Fargues-Scholze parameters on the generic locus, and construct a Fargues-Scholze-theoretic version of the BM-O bijection at generic parameters. △ Less

    Collective sliding relaxor ferroelectricity across thousand-layer van der Waals superstructures

    Authors: Nianze Shang, Cuiju Yu, Yucai Lin, Juan C. Arias-Muñoz, Jiajie Qi, Quanlin Guo, MD Gius Uddin, Youqiang Huang, Tuomas Pajunpää, Christopher P. Edwards, Rodolfo E. Canet-Albiach, Faisal Ahmed, Andreas C. Liapis, Yaoqiang Zhou, Zhibin Zhang, Hao Hong, Qing Dai, Andrea Alù, José L. Lado, Kaihui Liu, Zhipei Sun

    Abstract: …with negligible hysteresis in approximately 300-nm-thick crystals, corresponding to approximately 1000 atomic layers. The resulting electrostriction coefficient ($Q_{33}$ of approximately $1200\,\mathrm{m}^{4}\mathrm{C}^{-2}$) exceeds those of most crystalline electrostrictors by orders of magnitude. Unlike conventional relaxors, whose electromechanical res… ▽ More Relaxor ferroelectrics are among the most important electromechanical materials owing to their exceptionally large electrostrictive responses. However, conventional relaxor behavior is generally associated with chemical complexity and structural disorder, where local compositional fluctuations generate polar nanoregions, making the microscopic origin of this behavior difficult to control and engineer. Here we report sliding relaxor ferroelectricity (S-RFE), a previously unknown relaxor state in which engineered stacking-phase heterogeneity enables cooperative interlayer sliding to generate collective relaxor behavior. Through stacking-phase engineering in rhombohedral boron nitride, we create sliding domains that act as structural analogues of polar nanoregions and cooperatively align under an electric field through interlayer sliding. This collective state produces reversible electromechanical deformation of up to approximately $-1.6\%$ with negligible hysteresis in approximately 300-nm-thick crystals, corresponding to approximately 1000 atomic layers. The resulting electrostriction coefficient ($Q_{33}$ of approximately $1200\,\mathrm{m}^{4}\mathrm{C}^{-2}$) exceeds those of most crystalline electrostrictors by orders of magnitude. Unlike conventional relaxors, whose electromechanical response is often suppressed by dipolar freezing at low temperatures, S-RFE remains active down to 1.6 K and maintains giant electromechanical functionality. Our results establish sliding relaxor ferroelectricity as a new route to relaxor behavior and reveal how atomic-scale interlayer motion can be amplified into collective mesoscale functionality across approximately one thousand layers, providing a materials-by-design platform for electromechanical, photonic, and quantum technologies based on van der Waals materials. △ Less

    cs.CV

    OneScene-Bench: Do Vision-Language Models Integrate Evidence Across Views?

    Authors: Swathi Suhas, Anurag Das, Bernt Schiele, Jonas Fischer

    Abstract: …Existing multi-image evaluations do not isolate this capability, as tasks may admit correct solutions through independent per-view processing without requiring cross-view correspondence. We introduce OneScene-Bench, a controlled benchmark designed to explicitly distinguish when cross-view integration is necessary. Our benchmark evaluates three complementary… ▽ More Vision-language models (VLMs) increasingly process multiple views of the same scene, but it remains unclear whether they integrate these observations into a coherent scene-level representation. Existing multi-image evaluations do not isolate this capability, as tasks may admit correct solutions through independent per-view processing without requiring cross-view correspondence. We introduce OneScene-Bench, a controlled benchmark designed to explicitly distinguish when cross-view integration is necessary. Our benchmark evaluates three complementary capabilities on real-world multi-view scenes: unique object counting, cross-view consistency, and spatial reasoning. For counting, we control object overlap across views to separate cases solvable by simple per-view aggregation from those requiring instance-level correspondence. For consistency, we introduce localized edits in shared and view-specific regions, testing whether models distinguish genuine scene contradictions from differences caused by viewpoint and visibility. For spatial reasoning, we construct object relations that require combining spatial evidence across viewpoints to compare physical proximity. We evaluate twelve state-of-the-art open-weight and proprietary VLMs and find that counting accuracy drops substantially on complementary views, ranging from 4.5-50.0%, compared with 45.5-81.5% on complete-overlap examples. Moreover, cross-view consistency remains around 50% across models, including recent proprietary VLMs, despite substantially stronger performance on spatial reasoning. Our results reveal a substantial gap between processing multiple images and integrating evidence across them, highlighting the need for evaluations that explicitly test scene-level multi-view understanding. Code and data are available at https://github.com/swathisuhas/OneScene-Bench. △ Less

    Authors: Juan Bory-Reyes, Baruch Schneider, Diana Barseghyan Schneiderová, Yifan Zhang

    Abstract: …, and the corresponding Fischer theory uses right multiplication. For one vector, we determine the kernel and cokernel explicitly, including the additional singular monomial and… ▽ More We study the intrinsic right $q$-radial derivative at integral superdimension $M=m-2n$, using an independent formal parameter $Q$ before the specialisation $Q=q^M$. The exterior-blade action carries the parity factor $(-1)^{|I|}$, and the corresponding Fischer theory uses right multiplication. We establish finite-support compatibility, faithful coordinate realisations in a sufficient finite-block range, and localised Green and determinant-localised Fischer decompositions. For one vector, we determine the kernel and cokernel explicitly, including the additional singular monomial and missing image exactly when $M\in-2\mathbb N_0$. In degree zero the right Fischer operator is diagonal on auxiliary exterior blades, with eigenvalue $[M-p]_q+p$ at nonempty support rank $p$. For positive integral $M$, resonances in $0<q<1$ occur exactly at ranks $p>M$: each rank has one simple resonance, these values increase strictly with $p$, and the corresponding kernels and singular right-monogenic elements are explicit. The two-vector Fischer determinant is nonzero for positive $M$ and $0<q<1$. △ Less

    A Gaschütz Lemma for linear algebraic groups with applications to the Riemann-Hilbert correspondence

    Authors: Jason Bell, Michael Wibmer

    Abstract: …differential embedding problem over the rational function field has a regular singular solution. We also establish a purely algebraic variant of the Riemann-Hilbert correspondence on the punctured projective line that works over any countable algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. ▽ More The classical Gaschütz Lemma states that for a surjective morphism $G\to H$ of finite groups, generators $h_1,\ldots,h_d$ of $H$ can be lifted to generators $g_1,\ldots,g_d$ of $G$, provided that $G$ can be generated by $d$ elements. We prove an analog of this statement for linear algebraic groups. This has applications to the algebraic-geometric theory of linear differential equations: We show that every regular singular differential embedding problem over the rational function field has a regular singular solution. We also establish a purely algebraic variant of the Riemann-Hilbert correspondence on the punctured projective line that works over any countable algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. △ Less

    Szemerédi's Theorem Along Cantor Sets of Integers

    Authors: Alex Burgin, Anastasios Fragkos, Michael T. Lacey, Dario Mena, Maria Carmen Reguera

    Abstract: …is syndetic. By the Furstenberg correspondence principle, it follows that for every set of integers $A$ of positive upper Banach density there is a syndetic set $B$ of indices such that, for each $n \in B$, the set of $a$ with $a, a+k_n, \ldots, a+ \ell k_n \in A$ again has positive upper Banach density. A Cantor set contains an IP-set, so the existence o… ▽ More Let $\mathcal C= \{k_1<k_2 < \cdots\}$ be Cantor set of integers, that is a set of integers with restricted digits modulo a base $b$, and suppose $0$ is one of the restricted digits. We show that $$ \liminf_{M-N\to\infty}\mathbb E _{n\in [N]- [M]} m(A\cap T^{-k_n} A \cap \cdots \cap T^{-\ell k_n} A )>0. $$ The averages above are uniform in the index $n$, and so the set of indices $n$ for which $m(A\cap T^{-k_n} A \cap \cdots \cap T^{-\ell k_n} A )>0$ is syndetic. By the Furstenberg correspondence principle, it follows that for every set of integers $A$ of positive upper Banach density there is a syndetic set $B$ of indices such that, for each $n \in B$, the set of $a$ with $a, a+k_n, \ldots, a+ \ell k_n \in A$ again has positive upper Banach density. A Cantor set contains an IP-set, so the existence of such progressions, and the positivity of the density above for a single index, already follow from the IP Szemerédi theorem of Furstenberg and Katznelson, and from the work of Kra and Shalom. The point of this paper is instead the frequency with which the step sizes $k_n$ may be found, measured in the natural enumeration of the Cantor set itself. △ Less

Both classifications exhibit systemati… ▽ More Waves in solar magnetic elements provide a pathway for transporting and dissipating energy in the chromosphere, yet the relationship between photospheric oscillations and chromospheric spectral structure remains incompletely understood. We investigate whether distinct chromospheric Mg II spectral states are linked to different populations of photospheric kink mode oscillations, with particular focus on the role of high frequency (~5mHz) waves. Magnetic elements are tracked in SDO/HMI magnetograms to derive horizontal velocity power spectra, while corresponding Mg II h + k profiles from IRIS are classified using k-means clustering into morphology-based (SHAPE) and intensity-based (ABS) groups. Both classifications exhibit systematic differences in oscillatory behavior. The SHAPE clusters isolate spectral morphologies associated with enhanced high-frequency power, including cases where oscillations in the 4-6 mHz band exceed those in the photospheric p-mode band. In contrast, the ABS clusters show a monotonic increase in the relative contribution of high-frequency oscillations with increasing emission strength and magnetic flux. These results indicate that high-frequency oscillations are both selectively enhanced in specific chromospheric states and systematically strengthened in stronger magnetic environments. This suggests that magnetic structuring plays a key role in regulating both the transmission and the conversion of kink mode wave energy into observable chromospheric signatures. △ Less

    Abstract: …are inclined by 46° with respect to the line of sight. The slight misalignment between these two components suggests either precession of the ejection axis, a temporal change in the dominant mas… ▽ More We present a morpho-kinematic study of the Necklace Nebula based on spatially resolved spectroscopic observations and 3D modeling performed with the SHAPE software. The nebula is characterized by an equatorial disrupted ring accompanied by bipolar outflows. The geometry and velocity field of these different components are derived from the comparison between observed and synthetic images and position-velocity diagrams. The best model successfully reproduces the main morphological and kinematic features of the nebula, allowing us to constrain its 3D structure. We confirm that the disrupted ring is tilted about 60° from the plane of the sky, but the bipolar outflows are inclined by 46° with respect to the line of sight. This situation might indicate that the bipolar structures are not simple extensions of the equatorial ring, but instead correspond to a distinct mass-ejection event. The slight misalignment between these two components suggests either precession of the ejection axis, a temporal change in the dominant mass-loss geometry, or a misalignment in the magnetic field. Given that the Necklace Nebula hosts a close binary central star and is a well-established post-common envelope planetary nebula, our results support a scenario in which binary interaction plays a central role in shaping its morphology, even before the common envelope phase. △ Less

    The geometry of Floquet states

    Authors: Vladimir Y. Cherynak, John R. Klein

    Abstract: This paper studies the moduli space of "good" periodic Hamiltonians, i.e., periodic Hamiltonians with nondegenerate Floquet spectrum. The corresponding Floquet states determine a geometric model for the moduli space of such Hamiltonians. The model is expressed in terms of configuration spaces, flag manifolds, and principal bundles. A corollary of the… ▽ More This paper studies the moduli space of "good" periodic Hamiltonians, i.e., periodic Hamiltonians with nondegenerate Floquet spectrum. The corresponding Floquet states determine a geometric model for the moduli space of such Hamiltonians. The model is expressed in terms of configuration spaces, flag manifolds, and principal bundles. A corollary of the main result is the complete determination of the rational cohomology ring of the space of good periodic Hamiltonians. The paper also considers the adiabatic driving of a 1-parameter family of good periodic Hamiltonians. We formulate two conjectures describing the resulting asymptotic quantum evolution. These are Floquet analogues of the adiabatic theorem in quantum mechanics. △ Less

    Partitions in quantum theory

    Authors: Augustin Vanrietvelde, Octave Mestoudjian, Pablo Arrighi

    Abstract: …as sub-C*-algebras of the algebra of operators on the global system. However, most decompositional approaches have so far restricted their scope to the case of systems corresponding to factor algebras. We argue that this is a mistake: one should cater for the possibility for non-factor subsystems, arising for instance from symmetry considerations. Building o… ▽ More Decompositional theories describe the ways in which a global physical system can be split into subsystems, facilitating the study of how different possible partitions of the same system interplay, e.g. in terms of inclusions or signalling. In quantum theory, subsystems are usually framed as sub-C*-algebras of the algebra of operators on the global system. However, most decompositional approaches have so far restricted their scope to the case of systems corresponding to factor algebras. We argue that this is a mistake: one should cater for the possibility for non-factor subsystems, arising for instance from symmetry considerations. Building on simple examples, we motivate and present a definition of partitions into an arbitrary number of parts, each of which is a possibly non-factor sub-C*-algebra. We discuss its physical interpretation and study its properties, in particular with regard to the structure of algebras' centres. We prove that partitions, defined at the C*-algebraic level, can be represented in terms of a splitting of Hilbert spaces, using the framework of routed quantum circuits. For some partitions, however, such a representation necessarily retains a residual pseudo-nonlocality. We provide an example of this behaviour, given by the partition of a fermionic system into local modes. △ Less

Perpendicular incoming waves eliminate the entire local-area contribution, exposing a finite interaction mediated by the surrounding fluid. Reflection allows the incoming wave-number ratio to vary at exact resonance, revealing a sign-changing zero of this fluid coupling at \(λ_*\simeq1.469\). Uniform matching of the Gross--Pitaevskii (GP) four-wave coupling to the fluid result establishes microscopic zeros that approach this ratio at long wavelengths. Numerical calculations also show the corresponding sign change. Independent calculations in separate resonant geometries connect the coupling sign to the phase of coherently generated waves and the frequency ordering of reflection-even and reflection-odd modes. The surrounding flow thus determines whether an exactly resonant conversion channel is present at four-wave order. △ Less

Our results promote magnetic quivers to tools for the quantitative study of how the five-dimensional superconformal index responds to deformations. Once the parameter dictionary is known, exact three-dimensional observables can be used to reconstruct the corresponding five-dimensional physics and to navigate the full deformation network in both directions. △ Less

In spacetime with torsion, the Dirac equation becomes a nonlinear, cubic equation in the spinor wave function. Under charge conjugation, the nonlinear term changes sign relative to the mass term. A Dirac spinor and its charge conjugate therefore satisfy different field equations. We use the spinor connection formalism to derive the Dirac equation for a spherical spinor in its centrally symmetric gravitational field with torsion. We determine the energy$-$momentum tensor for this spinor and show that the energy contribution from torsion is negative for matter particles and positive for antimatter particles. Consequently, the ground state of a fermion has a lower energy than the ground state of the corresponding antifermion. We also derive the Einstein$-$Cartan$-$Dirac equations for this spinor; they indicate the existence of particle-like soliton solutions exhibiting matter$-$antimatter energy asymmetry. △ Less

The framework integrates raw data, estimate… ▽ More Graphical models are widely used to represent conditional independence structures, but learning them is challenging when data are fragmented across sources that measure different subsets of variables and may not be directly shareable. We develop a general framework for reconstructing a shared global graph from heterogeneous, partially observed sources. Our framework permits both the observed variable sets and source-specific distributions to differ. Under a source-wise faithfulness condition, local conditional independence graphs correspond to latent projections of the shared global graph, which is identifiable up to the equivalence class induced by the observed variable sets. The framework integrates raw data, estimated source graphs, or a combination of both, accommodating settings with restricted access to individual-level data, such as privacy constraints. We develop mixed-integer optimization formulations and scalable approximate algorithms for several classes of graphical models. Unlike standard high-dimensional analyses, our guarantees are governed by new combinatorial notions of signal strength that capture how information propagates across partially observed sources. Extensive simulations further show that integration can substantially improve estimation of source-specific projected graphs relative to separate source methods. Real-data application illustrates the framework's use with partially-observed sources of fatty acid measurements. △ Less

    Abstract: …bits of space, together with a corresponding $\tildeΩ\left(n^2/β^2\right)$ two-party communication lower bound [Halldórsson et al., ICALP'12], seeming to resolve the problem. However, their algorithm crucially relies on randomness, and the best known deterministic algorithm remains a folklore derandomisation using $O\left(n^2/β\right)$ bits of space, le… ▽ More We study the canonical \textsf{Maximum Clique} and \textsf{Maximum Independent Set} problems in the one-pass edge-arrival graph streaming setting. Here, the edges of some input graph $G = (V,E)$ are presented one at a time (possibly including deletions), before an algorithm needs to produce either a large clique or independent set at the end of the stream, with the focus being on space complexity. We are interested in finding $β$-approximate solutions, for any $β\geq 1$. Previous work gave an algorithm using $\tilde{O}\left(n^2/β^2\right)$ bits of space, together with a corresponding $\tildeΩ\left(n^2/β^2\right)$ two-party communication lower bound [Halldórsson et al., ICALP'12], seeming to resolve the problem. However, their algorithm crucially relies on randomness, and the best known deterministic algorithm remains a folklore derandomisation using $O\left(n^2/β\right)$ bits of space, leaving a (deterministic) gap of size $\tilde{O}(β)$. We make progress towards this gap with a strong lower bound for a restricted range of $β$: whenever $β\leq \sqrt{n}/36\log n$, any deterministic algorithm for either problem must use $Ω\left(\frac{n^2}{β\cdot\log n}\right)$ bits of space. Our proof is via a two-party one-way communication lower bound, and highlights the power of randomness when approaching either of these problems. △ Less

    Authors: Emanuele Contini, J. K. Jang, Jinsu Rhee, Changjo Seo, Sukyoung K. Yi

    Abstract: …larger scatter, particularly toward high host masses. We further show that BHOF and BH--host scaling relations encode complementary information. Using the YS200 cosmological simulation as our reference volume, we study the $M_{\rm BH}$--$M_{\star}$ and $M_{\rm BH}$--$M_{\rm bulge}$ relations for different BH mass selections and compare their redshift evolution with the hydrodynamical zoom-in simulation \texttt{NewCluster}. We find that BH mass cuts primarily affect the low-mass regime, while the selected relations progressively converge toward the intrinsic no-cut relations at higher host masses. The $M_{\rm BH}$--$M_{\star}$ relation also depends systematically on galaxy morphology, with increasingly bulge-dominated systems preferentially occupying higher stellar masses. Over $z=0$--$3$, FEGA25 shows no clear systematic evolution of either scaling relation, consistent with a broadly preserved statistical connection between BH and host-galaxy growth. NewCluster shows no clear monotonic evolution either, although it exhibits larger scatter, particularly toward high host masses. We further show that BHOF and BH--host scaling relations encode complementary information. A low BHOF generally corresponds to a stronger selection-induced offset, but the mapping is not unique: substantially different occupation fractions can yield similar median scaling relations, and at fixed BHOF the bias depends on the host property and underlying BH mass distribution. At $z=0$, \texttt{FEGA25} is consistent with available observational constraints for both stellar- and bulge-mass relations, while predicting increased scatter and bending toward lower host masses. △ Less

    Beyond fabrication: Misattributed numbers in papers written by AI research agents

    Authors: Mosab Alfaqeeh, Omar Hourani, Muath Hourani, Hashem Alfaqeeh

    Abstract: …claims from 18 submissions. Failures concentrate on alignment between the paper and the underlying work rather than overt cheating, and about four in five unsupported claims correspond to values in the agent's own files that come from the wrong experiment, metric, or run. We introduce a deterministic claim-to-evidence checker that scores whether the surr… ▽ More Verifying numerical claims in research written by AI agents is an important but challenging problem. Recent benchmarks show that many agent-written papers fail integrity checks, but such verdicts, often produced by a single LLM judge, identify failure without explaining its nature, and existing checks either test a paper for internal consistency or test whether a reported value appears somewhere in an agent's output. We re-analyze all 540 released judgments from a recent public benchmark of AI research agents (nine agents, fifteen open research problems) and manually label 256 numerical claims from 18 submissions. Failures concentrate on alignment between the paper and the underlying work rather than overt cheating, and about four in five unsupported claims correspond to values in the agent's own files that come from the wrong experiment, metric, or run. We introduce a deterministic claim-to-evidence checker that scores whether the surrounding context identifies the claimed result and calibrates each claim against numerical decoys. With its rules fixed before labeling, it certifies claims with over 90% precision against blinded annotators (78 of 85; Cohen's kappa = 0.89), while verifying only a subset of claims; most claims it cannot verify are nevertheless traceable, so failure to verify is not evidence of falsehood. An LLM verifier given the same evidence recovers more traceable claims but accepts about three times as many misattributed ones, so the two are best used together. Applied to 99 submissions, the checker reveals large differences in traceability across agents, and a second judge from a different vendor preserves the official agent ranking while passing twice as many papers. These findings motivate provenance-first auditing of AI-produced research. △ Less

Just as fusion categories have played an important role in understanding non-invertible symmetries, multi-fusion categories play an analogous role for the symmetries of such decomposing theories. We describe a finite symmetry as a multi-fusion category together with its action functor, which keeps track of trivially acting symmetries and of the topological interfaces between universes. Relative to a chosen symmetry, we classify pairs of universes by the topological interfaces it contains, which reflect the structure of the multi-fusion category: universes in different indecomposable summands are joined by no interface (Type~III), while within a summand they are related by invertible (Type~I) or only non-invertible (Type~II) interfaces. For orbifolds with a trivially acting abelian normal subgroup, we derive the full inherited symmetry, which characterizes the operators between universes found case by case by Robbins, Sharpe and Vandermeulen and yields pairs of Types~I and~II but never of Type~III. We construct Type~III pairs instead from extensions of $2$-group type and from renormalization group flows out of a common ultraviolet theory. Finally, we propose a correspondence with the generalized families of quantum field theories of Brennan and Intriligator, in which equivariant families correspond to Type~I pairs and categorical ones to Type~II pairs whose gauging preserves the symmetry, family anomalies appear as universes carrying different symmetry-protected topological phases, and Type~III pairs can realize families over a discrete parameter. An appendix by Xingyang Yu works out the higher-charge Schwinger model as a Lagrangian example. △ Less

    Abstract: …, yielding a rational function decomposition of the projected integer-point transform. We use this decomposition to provide an expli… ▽ More We present an analogue of Brion's theorem for the projected integer-point transform. Brion's theorem writes the integer-point transform of a rational polyhedron $P$ as the sum of the rational functions associated to its vertex cones. The pushforward $π_*$ along a lattice projection $π$ turns the transform of $P$ into the generating function of the numbers of lattice points in the fibres of $π$. We group the vertices of $P$ into contracted classes, generated by the edges whose direction $π$ kills, and show that the sum of the rational functions associated to the vertex cones in a contracted class can be pushed forward along $π$, yielding a rational function decomposition of the projected integer-point transform. For a lattice polytope, the classes correspond to the connected components of the fixed locus of the associated toric variety under the action of a subtorus, and our formula resembles the localization formula for this subtorus. We use this decomposition to provide an explicit formula for the projected integer-point transform of a linear family of polytopes. We illustrate this result with families of Gelfand--Zetlin and string polytopes. △ Less

    Authors: Sergio Javier González Manrique, Juan Carlos Trelles Arjona, Christoph Kuckein, Azaymi L. Siu-Tapia, Fernando Moreno-Insertis, Samrat Sen, Sami K.     Abstract: …extreme-ultraviolet (EUV) brightenings in the surrounding emerging system. Multiline spectropolarimetric inversions reveal temperatures between approximately 4000 and 10,000 K and a smooth variation of magnetic field inclination along… ▽ More Emerging flux regions (EFRs) transport magnetic flux and release energy in the solar atmosphere, often hosting arch filament systems (AFSs). We present high-resolution observations of an EFR obtained with Sunrise III, combining the Tunable Magnetograph (TuMag), the Sunrise Chromospheric Infrared Spectro-Polarimeter (SCIP), and the Solar Dynamics Observatory (SDO). The dataset follows an AFS continuously for nearly four hours. Line-of-sight (LOS) velocities derived from the Mg I b$_2$ line reveal three episodic plasma-flow events, with downflows reaching approximately 17 km s$^{-1}$ at the footpoints and enhanced upflows near the loop tops. Although this pattern agrees with the classical emergence and draining scenario, the strongest upflows and downflows occur nearly simultaneously. Atmospheric Imaging Assembly (AIA) observations at 304 and 171 Angstrom reveal transient extreme-ultraviolet (EUV) brightenings in the surrounding emerging system. Some occur during enhanced-flow episodes, while others do not, indicating that the temporal correspondence is not systematic. Multiline spectropolarimetric inversions reveal temperatures between approximately 4000 and 10,000 K and a smooth variation of magnetic field inclination along a representative AFS loop. Non-force-free-field extrapolations provide the large-scale magnetic context of the low-lying AFS loops and surrounding coronal structures. Together, the observations reveal a spatially and temporally complex evolution without establishing a direct causal connection between local plasma-flow enhancements and EUV brightenings. △ Less

Thus, the pretrained Transformer is able to achieve faster rates of con… ▽ More We study in-context learning problems where a Transformer is pretrained on tasks drawn from a mixture distribution $π=\sum_{α\in\mathcal{A}} λ_α π_α$, called the pretraining prior, in which each mixture component $π_α$ is a distribution on tasks of a specific difficulty level indexed by $α$. Our goal is to understand the performance of the pretrained Transformer when evaluated on a different test distribution $μ$, consisting of tasks of fixed difficulty $β\in\mathcal{A}$, and with potential distribution shift relative to $π_β$, subject to the chi-squared divergence $χ^2(μ,π_β)$ being at most $κ$. In particular, we consider nonparametric regression problems with random smoothness, and multi-index models with both random smoothness and random effective dimension. We prove that a large Transformer pretrained on sufficient data achieves the optimal rate of convergence corresponding to the difficulty level $β$, uniformly over test distributions $μ$ in the chi-squared divergence ball. Thus, the pretrained Transformer is able to achieve faster rates of convergence on easier tasks and is robust to distribution shift at test time. Finally, we prove that even if an estimator had access to the test distribution $μ$, the convergence rate of its expected risk over $μ$ could not be faster than that of our pretrained Transformers, thereby providing a more appropriate optimality guarantee than minimax lower bounds. △ Less

This paper presents a curriculum-driven proximal policy optimization (PPO) framework for three-dimensional rocket landing in a stochastic environment incorporating altitude-dependent aerodynamic drag, wind shear, temporally correlated turbulence, discrete gusts, uncertain propulsion and aerodynamic parameters, thrust-vector misalignment, engine lag, actuator-gain variation, noisy observations, and command latency. Success decreases from 98.84\% under zero-disturbance conditions to 88.07\% in the highest weather-severity band. Successful touchdowns exhibit small miss distances and low residual horizontal and vertical velocities. An exploratory logistic-regression analysis identifies mean wind speed as the strongest measured correlate of success, with initial distance, initial altitude, initial vertical velocity, and wind-shear exponent also associated with outcomes. The results quantify both the effectiveness of the learned policy over the evaluated disturbance distribution and the degradation that occurs under severe compound atmospheric conditions. △ Less

    The Reality Gap in Automated Driving Systems Testing: Landscape, Challenges, and Research Directions

    Authors: Stefano Carlo Lambertenghi, Oliver Weissl, Johannes Betz, Andrea Stocco

    Abstract: …discrepancies between simulation and reality can undermine testing conclusions. It remains unclear which discrepancies the literature addresses and what evidence supports correspondence claims. We present a systematic mapping of 235 primary studies that measure or mitigate the reality gap in ADS testing, selected from 4,195 publications. We characterise obje… ▽ More Simulation is essential for testing automated driving systems (ADS), but discrepancies between simulation and reality can undermine testing conclusions. It remains unclear which discrepancies the literature addresses and what evidence supports correspondence claims. We present a systematic mapping of 235 primary studies that measure or mitigate the reality gap in ADS testing, selected from 4,195 publications. We characterise objectives, targets, approaches, experimental setups, and evaluation evidence. Our classification distinguishes simulated and real-world execution across six ADS test components, separately for each approach and its evaluation reference. This distinguishes real-world information used to construct an approach from evidence used to evaluate its claims. Of the included studies, 76% address mitigation, primarily targeting scenario and actor realism and perception, while 94% report validation or measurement procedures. Only 61 studies report metrics from multiple families covering sensor/perception correspondence, ADS behaviour, vehicle dynamics, or safety outcomes; this coverage does not establish causal links between discrepancies and test verdicts. The synthesis highlights that real-world content and physical execution must be interpreted against the claim evaluated. The findings motivate claim-specific evaluation and component-level reporting to establish which discrepancies affect testing conclusions and when simulation evidence supports conclusions about real-world ADS behaviour. △ Less

However, scene-level video queries can become ambiguous when multiple sounding objects are visible, particularly when the target and interferer are semantically similar. We propose SelectSep, a target-selective VQSS framework that jointly uses text and a single click coordinate to specify the target source and instance without requiring segmentation at inference time. We adapt the visual encoder through target-feature distillation with text and click conditioning, and then fine-tune the separator using the adapted visual features. We also introduce an on-screen interference setting, where both the target and interferer are visible, to evaluate target selectivity. Experiments on standard benchmarks and the on-screen interference setting demonstrate consistent improvements in separation quality and target selectivity, particularly for mixtures containing semantically similar sources. △ Less

We complete this investigation by including the Lorentz transformation that yields the proper frame for some of… ▽ More A procedure to determine the initial ansatz for the co-frame and spin connection characterizing a Riemann-Cartan geometry respecting a given group of continuous symmetries is illustrated. Assuming invariance of the metric under a particular group of symmetries, this gauge covariant Lie derivative when applied to the metric and associated orthonormal co-frame determines the values of an antisymmetric compensating matrix. The gauge covariant Lie derivative of the spin connection depends on this matrix, which when solved yields the spin connection compatible with the chosen co-frame. The procedure is straightforward and can be employed for any Riemann-Cartan geometry having symmetries including those with a non-trivial isotropy subgroup. Here we illustrate the procedure with numerous examples, including, spherically symmetric, plane symmetric, locally rotationally symmetric Bianchi type III, Gödel, de Sitter and anti-de Sitter geometries. Further, we have also solved the zero curvature constraint to obtain the resulting spin connection for the corresponding metric teleparallel geometry having this same symmetry group. We complete this investigation by including the Lorentz transformation that yields the proper frame for some of these metric teleparallel geometries. This investigation provides a kinematical framework useful for the further study of Poincaré Gauge and metric teleparallel theories of gravity. △ Less

    Authors: Roberto La Scala, Marco Marchesin, Sharwan K. Tiwari

    Abstract: …and algebraic solving.
