Reptile numerically outperforms STRAINER under a small iteration budget, although their pretraining budgets are not matched. As the number of iterations increases, the differences between the schemes shrink. STRAINER pretraining takes 39-40 minutes, compared with 44.98 hours for Reptile. △ Less

    Abstract: …admitted by systems of partial differential equations (PDEs) induce Poisson bivectors on suitable finite-dimensional subsystems describing invariant solutions. We show that the induced Poisson bivectors relate constants o… ▽ More We establish that local Hamiltonian operators admitted by systems of partial differential equations (PDEs) induce Poisson bivectors on suitable finite-dimensional subsystems describing invariant solutions. This correspondence is governed by the mechanism of invariant reduction, which applies uniformly to point, contact, and higher symmetries. We show that the induced Poisson bivectors relate constants of invariant motion to symmetries of such subsystems. Applying the same reduction mechanism to invariant conservation laws, we further deduce that the corresponding Poisson brackets on the reduced systems agree, up to a global sign, with those of the original systems. The results are illustrated by two examples in which the induced Poisson brackets and constants of invariant motion yield integrability of the reduced systems. The construction is independent of the choice of an $\ell$-normal inclusion of a PDE system into jet spaces, with the corresponding odd cotangent covering being $\ell$-normal. △ Less

    Authors: Zakhar Kabluchko

    Abstract: …volumes of the closed positive hulls of deterministic Ornstein--Uhlenbeck spirals and their circular analogues in Hilbert spaces. After suitable rescaling, the small-time limits of these conic intrinsic volumes recover the formulas of Gao--Vitale and Gao for… ▽ More Let $(U_t)_{t\in\mathbb R}$ be a stationary Ornstein--Uhlenbeck process in $\mathbb R^d$ whose coordinate processes $(U_t^{(i)})_{t\in\mathbb R}$, $1\le i\le d$, are independent, centered, and have covariance function $\mathbb E [U_s^{(i)}U_t^{(i)}]=e^{-|t-s|/2}$. For every $T>0$, we obtain an explicit formula for the probability that the convex hull $\mathrm{conv}\{U_t:0\le t\le T\}$ contains the origin. We obtain a similar formula for a circular Ornstein--Uhlenbeck loop whose covariance is the normalized $T$-periodization of this kernel. For each fixed dimension $d$, we determine the precise long-time asymptotics of the non-absorption probabilities, which decay as explicit constants times $T^{d-1} e^{-T/2}$ as $T\to\infty$. We also show that, as $d\to\infty$, the absorption probabilities in both models undergo a transition from $0$ to $1$ around $T=2d$, with a common Gaussian limiting profile on the scale $\sqrt d$. We also obtain explicit formulas for the probability that $σZ$ belongs to the convex hull of Brownian motion or Brownian bridge on $[0,1]$ in $\mathbb R^d$, where $Z$ is an independent standard Gaussian vector and $σ\ge0$. The large-$σ$ asymptotics recover the known formulas for the expected volumes of these convex hulls. Finally, we compute the conic intrinsic volumes of the closed positive hulls of deterministic Ornstein--Uhlenbeck spirals and their circular analogues in Hilbert spaces. Their Grassmann angles coincide with the corresponding Ornstein--Uhlenbeck absorption probabilities. After suitable rescaling, the small-time limits of these conic intrinsic volumes recover the formulas of Gao--Vitale and Gao for the intrinsic volumes of the Brownian body and its Brownian-bridge analogue. △ Less

Supporting their review with language model agents requires discovering unstated deficiencies across related documents, acquiring missing company facts, and verifying that requested revisions satisfy evidence-backed requirements. Existing financial question-answering evaluations provide limited evidence of these interactive capabilities. We introduce \textbf{FilingLoop}, an interactive benchmark and a self-evolving agent framework for corporate disclosure review and revision. Grounded in historical U.S.\ Securities and Exchange Commission correspondence, FilingLoop reconstructs 380 predominantly IPO review tasks from 96 companies under distinct information constraints. An evaluator separate from the acting agents scores final filings and responses against 6,099 eligible requirements and audits unsupported claims and harmful revisions. Our framework couples iterative review and revision within each case with procedural learning across cases, converting requirement-level evaluation feedback into role-specific guidance without updating model weights. Updates affect only subsequent cases, allowing procedural adaptation to be assessed without retrying scored tasks. On the evaluated stream, evolution increases requirement fulfillment from 24.28\% to 27.96\% and full comment resolution from 16.90\% to 20.75\%, with a 5.8\% increase in total cost over the fixed-policy variant. FilingLoop connects the concerns an agent discovers with the company facts it obtains and the revisions it commits, providing requirement-level feedback for procedures used in later review tasks. △ Less

We further prove that all twisted Fourier transforms are Weyl tr… ▽ More A unified Fourier framework for quantum weight enumerators of prime-power-dimensional qudits is developed in this paper. We introduce the notions of Weyl power spectrum and Weyl-conjugation autocorrelation for the Weyl-operator quantities nderlying the Shor--Laflamme weight enumerators. Representing these quantities as formal vectors, we show that they form a symplectic Fourier transform pair, from which the quantum MacWilliams identity follows by applying the Hamming-weight functional. This framework extends to a family of twisted Fourier transforms, including the Rains shadow transform. We show that identical-copy Bell sampling corresponds to a twisted autocorrelation related to the shadow enumerator through Weyl translation and Hamming projection. We further prove that all twisted Fourier transforms are Weyl translations of either the ordinary MacWilliams transform or the Rains shadow transform. This two-class reduction implies that the twists yield no additional independent coefficientwise positivity constraints under compatible projections. Nevertheless, the framework leads to refined MacWilliams and shadow identities that retain more Fourier information than ordinary Hamming weight enumerators. We employ these refined identities in linear programming, together with simplex mapping and shadow-coset counting, to exclude surviving candidate weight distributions. These techniques yield tighter bounds for binary and qutrit quantum codes, including nonexistence results for specific code parameters. △ Less

Function-level benchmarks offer limited visibility into cross-function relationships, while whole-program benchmarks typically evaluate final outcomes of tasks that may be solvable from local clues alone, making it difficult to disentangle interprocedural reasoning from exploration, tool use, execution, and retrieval. To address this limitation, we introduce ICALL-BENCH, a benchmark that directly evaluates whether LLMs and agents can understand and reason over interprocedural semantics in binary programs, using indirect-call target recovery as a measurable probe. We evaluate five recent LLMs and their corresponding agents. Our results reveal a wide separation across current models and agents, with program-level verifiable success rates ranging from 23% to 81%. They also show that greater information access does not consistently lead to better results, with particularly limited gains on the hardest cases, where deeper interprocedural reasoning remains the primary bottleneck.     The Geometry of Quantum Teleportation in Phase Space

    Authors: Jasel Berra-Montiel, Alberto Molgado, Mar Sánchez-Córdova

    Abstract: We formulate the standard quantum teleportation protocol within the framework of deformation quantization, employing the Stratonovich--Weyl (SW) correspondence and the associated star product on the coadjoint orbits of $SU(2)$. Each qubit is represented by a spherical phase space $S^2$ endowed with the Kirillov--Kostant--Souriau (KKS) symplectic structure. W… ▽ More We formulate the standard quantum teleportation protocol within the framework of deformation quantization, employing the Stratonovich--Weyl (SW) correspondence and the associated star product on the coadjoint orbits of $SU(2)$. Each qubit is represented by a spherical phase space $S^2$ endowed with the Kirillov--Kostant--Souriau (KKS) symplectic structure. Within this setting, we explicitly construct the phase space representations of the entangled Bell resource, the controlled-NOT and Hadamard operators, the projective measurements, and the conditional Pauli corrections. We demonstrate how the quantum information initially encoded in the Wigner function for the input qubit is redistributed through bipartite and tripartite phase space correlations. Upon consideration of a conditional unitary transformation, the measurement outcome produces a Wigner function on the receiver's coadjoint orbit that is related to the input by an outcome-dependent rotation, which can subsequently be inverted through the implementation of an appropriate Pauli correction. The covariance of the SW correspondence provides a geometric interpretation of this process, in which quantum information is redistributed and reconstructed without transferring the underlying symplectic geometry. Our formulation can also be extended to study quantum teleportation in more general phase space geometries equipped with suitable star products. △ Less

Their emergence has been extensively investigated with a particular emphasis on the role of network topology, both in conventional pairwise interactions and, more recently, in higher-order interaction networks. Here, we propose a generalized framework for neuronal hypergraphs incorporating higher-order interactions through attractive chemical and repulsive electrical coupling. Using networks of FitzHugh--Nagumo neurons with interaction orders d=2,3,4, corresponding to (d+1)-body interactions within a framework that can be extended to higher d, we numerically investigate the emergence of chimera states under the combined effect of attractive and repulsive coupling and we compare the resulting dynamics with those of their corresponding clique-projected networks. Our results reveal that, both dynamical similarities and qualitative differences between genuine higher-order interactions on hypergraphs and their pairwise representations. While higher-order interactions often support broader chimera regions, we show that clique projections can also preserve or promote chimera states, even in regimes where the corresponding hypergraphs exhibit fully incoherent dynamics. Therefore, these findings suggest that chimera formation is not only influenced by the topology or by the interaction order but also by the form of the coupling functions and the balance between attractive and repulsive interactions, offering further insights into collective dynamics in neuronal networks. △ Less

    Submitted 27 October, 2026; v1 submitted 22 October, 2026; originally announced October 2026.

    cs.LG cs.AI cs.CL

    Interpretability Is Not Just a Tax: Sparsity and Diversity Yield Legible and Composable Reward Experts for Personalization

    Authors: Yifan Wang, Jinyi Mu, Mayank Jobanputra, Yu Wang, Ji-Ung Lee, Soyoung Oh, Isabel Valera, Vera Demberg

    Abstract: …What these components actually represent, however, is rarely examined. We audit Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) reward models along three rungs---semantic coherence, functional correspondence, and composability---in controlled and real-world settings, holding the architecture and training data fixed so that differences isolate the effect of the training objective.… ▽ More Preference modeling aims to capture heterogeneous human values and increasingly relies on learning latent components to represent this variation without fine-grained supervision. What these components actually represent, however, is rarely examined. We audit Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) reward models along three rungs---semantic coherence, functional correspondence, and composability---in controlled and real-world settings, holding the architecture and training data fixed so that differences isolate the effect of the training objective. Our diagnosis is that the components conventional MoE reward models learn are entangled, which limits not only interpretability but also the personalization such models are built for. The entanglement is not intrinsic to the architecture but a property of the training objective: off-the-shelf sparsity and diversity regularizers already substantially reduce it. The resulting configuration, which we call sparse MoE, recovers all underlying semantic categories without domain supervision in a controlled setting, reaching 84.8% expert purity against 60.8% for conventional MoE; on real-world data its experts admit human-interpretable task- and domain-level descriptions aligned with their functional strengths. Targeted interventions on routing weights produce predictable preference changes, showing that these interpretations are behaviorally grounded rather than merely descriptive. Crucially, the disentanglement induced by this objective also makes the experts composable: lightweight router updates yield a 25.81-point improvement in few-shot personalization with 50 examples, roughly 2.7 times the gain of the identical architecture and adaptation procedure applied to entangled experts. Together, these results show that MoE reward models can learn interpretable expert structure, and that the same structure can substantially improve personalization. △ Less

For each of these cases, we begin by introducing the Dunkl derivative into the standard commutative phase space to evaluate the reflection parity symmetries. Then, using the Seiberg-Witten map, we extend the generalized system to a non-commutative phase space. In both cases, we determine the eigenfunctions and the energy spectrum exactly (which correspond to generalized Landau levels when a magnetic field is present). Furthermore, we compute the non-relativistic limit of the system and graphically analyze the behavior of the energy spectra. Our results demonstrate that the combination of the Dunkl deformation parameters, the non-commutative phase space geometry, and the magnetic field produces a parity-dependent modification of the energy levels. Finally, we verify that when the external magnetic field or the non-commutative parameters vanish, our exact solutions correctly reduce to the commutative Dunkl models established in previous works. △ Less

Executable validation establishes feasibility, yet does not reveal how a task behaves relative to a given solver setting. In this paper, we present CalibForge, an autonomous terminal-task synthesis system that uses verified solver behavior to revise candidate tasks through adversarial solver calibration. Multi-solver calibration targets disagreement within a heterogeneous solver pool, whereas contrastive solver calibration targets a designated strong-pass/weak-fail relation; both operationalize a solver-relative learnable zone anchored in demonstrated solvability. Using CalibForge, we construct 5,431 calibrated terminal tasks. Our ablations show that both strategies yield more effective supervision than authoring and validation alone or ordinary single-solver feedback. Models trained on the full collection achieve 31.84% and 47.57% on Terminal-Bench 2.1. The largest improvements over the corresponding base model reach 23.97 percentage points on Terminal-Bench 2.1, 26.54 points on SWE-bench Pro, and 30.04 points on Doc2Repo. Together, these results support solver-relative learnability as a practical target for constructing effective and transferable agent training data. △ Less

This study examines how the construction of the desired wrist position affects whether computed arm joint angles reach their permitted limits on a Unitree H1-2. A reconstructed target is formed from demonstrated arm directions and robot segment lengths, so its shoulder-to-wrist distance depends on the demonstrated angle between the segments even when no posture-matching preference is applied. Across a forward-reach sweep the reconstructed target places a joint angle at a bound in 10 of 20 repetitions and the reach-normalized target in none. Across 25 recordings from one participant the corresponding counts are 8 and 6 on the reaching arm and 14 and 5 on the supporting arm, and a second participant repeats the bricklaying and lifting comparison. In hardware replay a joint is commanded to a bound in 12 of 16 direction-matching arm-records and in none of the 16 normalized records, and within the eligible records both tracking error and executed torque at those frames exceed their values elsewhere in the same run. Cross-body reach and arm-to-arm clearance identify limits of radial adjustment. The findings support treating target construction and posture matching as separate retargeting decisions, and evaluating both alongside the wrist positions and clearance that manipulation requires. △ Less

    Spokes, not a Ring: Revisiting the Weekday Manifold in Language Models

    Authors: Diksha Gupta, Rieke Fruengel, Stefan Heimersheim

    Abstract: …some concepts as multi-dimensional manifolds instead of single directions. Using these manifolds to interpret or steer representations often assumes that moving along them corresponds to a continuous change in the represented concept. In this work, we test this continuous manifold assumption on the prototypical weekday manifold. Despite its striking circular… ▽ More Recent work has hypothesised that large language models may represent some concepts as multi-dimensional manifolds instead of single directions. Using these manifolds to interpret or steer representations often assumes that moving along them corresponds to a continuous change in the represented concept. In this work, we test this continuous manifold assumption on the prototypical weekday manifold. Despite its striking circular geometry, we find that the weekday representations behave more like seven discrete states arranged in a circle than a continuous ring. First, we show that local sensitivity has seven minima aligned with the weekdays, producing a step-like mapping between representation and behaviour under on-manifold steering. Second, we test whether any concepts are represented along the manifold outside the seven discrete states, and find neither time of day, uncertainty, nor natural text populate the intermediate space. Together, these results challenge the continuous interpretation of the weekday manifold and motivate testing whether movement along other representation manifolds correspond to meaningful changes in model representations and behaviour. △ Less

To search for circumplanetary material associated with these candidates, we have obtained the most sensitive ($\sim 20$ $μ$Jy/beam rms) and highest angular resolution ($\sim 30$ mas) ALMA Band 7 (0.88 mm) observations of AB Aur to date. We recover the asymmetric dust ring in the continuum emission peaking at $\sim 0.9$ arcsec ($\sim 140$ au) and compact emission at the stellar position, but detect no point source at the location of AB Aur b or any of the other proposed protoplanet candidates down to a $3σ$ limit of $60$ $μ$Jy. For optically thin emission, this corresponds to a dust mass of $\lesssim 0.0059 M_\oplus$ (for a dust temperature of 40 K), comparable to the circumplanetary disk around PDS 70 c. The $^{12}$CO emission fills the continuum cavity, revealing inner spiral structure and a clear rotation pattern. The gas at the position of AB Aur b is smoothly connected to the ambient disk with no localized excess or obvious kinematic deviation. If any proposed candidates are accreting, their circumplanetary material must have a low dust mass, a small physical size, dust that is optically thick, or a dearth of large mm-emitting grains. △ Less

W… ▽ More On two-dimensional Anti-de Sitter space, Majorana fermions with a discrete set of finely tuned, non-zero mass parameters possess conserved spinor--tensors that are symmetric and traceless. In complex coordinates, these correspond to pairs of holomorphic and anti-holomorphic half-integer spin currents and admit Laurent series mode expansions reminiscent of standard two-dimensional conformal field theories. We exploit this fact in order to fully parameterise the classical phase space associated with the equation of motion in terms of arbitrary (anti-)holomorphic functions. We further show that this parameterisation can be written covariantly in terms of a conformal Killing spinor--tensor, hinting at a hidden conformal symmetry. We also show they possess chiral algebras which are strict subalgebras of the one present in the corresponding theory on flat space. △ Less

    Field theory of competing phases in the interacting Kitaev chain

    Authors: Pradip Kattel, Thierry Giamarchi

    Abstract: …fermionic representation in which this topological bulk sector is described instead by conventional symmetry breaking. This gives a continuum version of the SPT--SSB correspondence between different fermionic representations. Overall, our results provide an analytical description of one-dimensional interacting topological superconductors, from the competitio… ▽ More We study the interacting Kitaev chain with nearest-neighbor interactions and finite chemical potential using a combination of bosonization and density-matrix renormalization-group calculations. Focusing away from special exactly solvable lattice limits, we develop a continuum field-theoretic description of the generic finite-density interacting regime, where doping nonperturbatively modifies the competition between superconducting pairing and commensurate charge ordering. We show that the phase diagram consists of an intermediate incommensurate Luttinger liquid between the topological superconducting and commensurate charge-density-wave Mott phases, as well as a trivial band-insulating regime at large chemical potential. The commensurate--incommensurate and incommensurate--topological-superconducting phase boundaries are governed by Pokrovsky--Talapov and Berezinskii--Kosterlitz--Thouless criticality, respectively. We analyze the interacting topological superconducting regime and its boundary excitations. At the semiclassical level, and explicitly in the free-fermion boundary theory, the topological phase supports exponentially localized Majorana edge modes at interfaces between topological and trivial regions. We also construct a dual continuum fermionic representation in which this topological bulk sector is described instead by conventional symmetry breaking. This gives a continuum version of the SPT--SSB correspondence between different fermionic representations. Overall, our results provide an analytical description of one-dimensional interacting topological superconductors, from the competition between topology and commensurability and the resulting quantum criticality to the dual symmetry-breaking description of the topological phase. △ Less

    MoireKP: An Automated Workflow for Symmetry-Constrained Moiré Continuum Models

    Authors: Yan Zhang, Hanqi Pi, Wangqian Miao, Zhong Fang, Hongming Weng, Xi Dai, B. Andrei Bernevig, Quansheng Wu

    Abstract: …basis. These representations determine the symmetry-allowed Hamiltonian terms, whose coefficients are obtained by projecting the low-energy effective Hamiltonian onto the corresponding operator basis. The model dimension, localized reference states, and harmonic cutoff can be selected automatically or specified by the user. This Hamiltonian-based constructio… ▽ More First-principles Hamiltonians contain the electronic information needed to study moiré materials, but their size makes subsequent calculations costly. We present MoireKP, an automated workflow for constructing compact continuum models from localized-orbital ab initio Hamiltonians. The workflow constructs a valley-resolved truncated atomic-plane-wave (TAPW) Hamiltonian, selects a low-energy basis, and projects the crystal-symmetry operations into this basis. These representations determine the symmetry-allowed Hamiltonian terms, whose coefficients are obtained by projecting the low-energy effective Hamiltonian onto the corresponding operator basis. The model dimension, localized reference states, and harmonic cutoff can be selected automatically or specified by the user. This Hamiltonian-based construction retains wave-function information within the selected low-energy subspace. We apply the workflow to AA-stacked twisted bilayer MoTe$_2$, AB-stacked twisted bilayer ZrS$_2$, and A--AB twisted trilayer MoTe$_2$, covering K, M, and $Γ$ valleys. All models reproduce TAPW band energies within 3.0 meV in the target windows along high-symmetry $k$ paths. △ Less
